Stability threshold of Couette flow for Boussinesq equations in ℝ 2 \mathbb{R}^{2}
Yubo Chen, Wendong Wang, Guoxu Yang

Abstract
. This paper establishes the asymptotic stability threshold for the Couette flow ( y , 0 ) (y,0) under the 2D Boussinesq system in ℝ 2 \mathbb{R}^{2} . It was proved that for initial perturbations in Sobolev spaces with controlled low horizontal frequencies, the stability threshold is at most { 1 3 + , 2 3 + } \left\{\frac{1}{3}+,\frac{2}{3}+\right\} , extending the known threshold results from the periodic case 𝕋 x × ℝ y \mathbb{T}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{y} to the whole space. The core innovations are twofold: First, the ⟨ D x − 1 ⟩ \langle D_{x}^{-1}\rangle control on the initial data simultaneously resolves horizontal frequency singularities and optimizes integral indices when applying Young’s convolution inequality. Second, we develop a modified multiplier ℳ 3 \mathcal{M}_{3} that effectively absorbs the | D x | 1 / 3 |D_{x}|^{1/3} derivative structure induced by the temperature equation while handling nonlinear echo cascades.



1.
Introduction

In this paper, we investigate the nonlinear stability of the Couette flow
(y,0)
for the two-dimensional Boussinesq equations in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}v-\nu\Delta v+v\cdot\nabla v+\nabla p=\vartheta e_{2},\\
\partial_{t}\vartheta-\mu\Delta\vartheta+v\cdot\nabla\vartheta=0,\\
\operatorname{div}v=0,\\
\left.v\right|_{t=0}=v_{\text{in}}(x,y),\quad\left.\vartheta\right|_{t=0}=\vartheta_{\text{in}}(x,y),\end{array}\right.

where
v(t,x,y)=\left(v^{1},v^{2}\right)
denotes the velocity field,
\vartheta
the temperature and
p
the pressure. Moreover,
e_{2}=(0,1)^{T}
,
\nu
is the viscosity coefficient and
\mu
is the thermal diffusivity.

The Boussinesq approximation simplifies fluid dynamics by assuming that density variations are negligible except in the buoyancy term (typically due to temperature or concentration gradients). The Boussinesq approximation and Boussinesq equations provide a balanced approach to modeling buoyancy-driven flows by simplifying density variations while retaining essential physics. They are fundamental in studying thermal convection, environmental flows, and heat transfer where gravitational effects dominate; see
[
63
,
28
]
. Consider the perturbation of the system (
1
) under the Couette flow
(y,0)
and the constant temperature, and
letting
u=v-(y,0)
and
\theta=\vartheta-a
, where
a\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u-\nu\Delta u+y\partial_{x}u+\left(u^{2},\,0\right)+u\cdot\nabla u+\nabla p=(\theta+a)e_{2},\\
\partial_{t}\theta-\mu\Delta\theta+y\partial_{x}\theta+u\cdot\nabla\theta=0,\\
\operatorname{div}u=0,\\
\left.u\right|_{t=0}=u_{\text{in}}(x,y),\quad\left.\theta\right|_{t=0}=\theta_{\text{in}}(x,y).\end{array}\right.

Define
\omega=\partial_{y}u^{1}-\partial_{x}u^{2}
be the vorticity, and it follows that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\omega-\nu\Delta\omega+y\partial_{x}\omega+u\cdot\nabla\omega=\partial_{x}\theta,\\
\partial_{t}\theta-\mu\Delta\theta+y\partial_{x}\theta+u\cdot\nabla\theta=0,\\
u=\nabla^{\perp}(-\Delta)^{-1}\omega=\left(-\partial_{y},\partial_{x}\right)(-\Delta)^{-1}\omega,\\
\left.\omega\right|_{t=0}=\omega_{\mathrm{in}}(x,y),\quad\left.\theta\right|_{t=0}=\theta_{\mathrm{in}}(x,y).\end{array}\right.

(1.5)

When
\vartheta=0
, (
1
) is reduced to the famous Navier-Stokes equations.
The transition threshold problem was formulated by Bedrossian, Germain and Masmoudi
[
4
]
as follows:
Given a norm
\|\cdot\|_{X}
, determine a
\beta=\beta(X)
so that

\displaystyle\left\|u_{0}\right\|_{X}\ll Re^{-\gamma}\Rightarrow\text{ stability, }

\displaystyle\left\|u_{0}\right\|_{X}\gg Re^{-\gamma}\Rightarrow\text{ instability. }

The exponent
\gamma
is referred to as the transition threshold.
Similarly, for the Boussinesq system, it is interesting to consider the following stability threshold problem:

Given norms
\|\cdot\|_{Y_{1}}
and
\|\cdot\|_{Y_{2}}
, find
\alpha=\alpha\left(Y_{1},Y_{2}\right)
and
\beta=\beta\left(Y_{1},Y_{2}\right)
such that

\displaystyle\left\|u_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{Y_{1}}\leq\nu^{\alpha}\text{ and }\left\|\theta_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{Y_{2}}\leq\nu^{\beta}\Rightarrow\text{ stability, }

\displaystyle\left\|u_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{Y_{1}}\gg\nu^{\alpha}\text{ or }\left\|\theta_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{Y_{2}}\gg\nu^{\beta}\Rightarrow\text{ instability. }

For
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
, Deng–Wu–Zhang
[
28
]
proved the asymptotic stability of the steady state if the initial perturbations
(u_{\mathrm{in}},\theta_{\mathrm{in}})
satisfy

\left\|u_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{H^{2}}\leq\epsilon_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\left\|\theta_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{H^{1}}+\left\|\left|D_{x}\right|^{\frac{1}{6}}\theta_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{H^{1}}\leq\epsilon_{1}\nu^{\frac{11}{12}}.

(1.6)

The threshold obtained was improved by Zhang–Zi
[
63
]
to

\left\|u_{\text{in}}\right\|_{H^{s+1}}\leq\epsilon_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}},\quad\left\|\theta_{\text{in}}\right\|_{H^{s}}+\nu^{\frac{1}{6}}\left\|\left|D_{x}\right|^{\frac{1}{3}}\theta_{\text{in}}\right\|_{H^{s}}\leq\epsilon_{1}\nu^{\frac{5}{6}},

where
s>7
. Later, the threshold was improved again by Niu–Zhao
[
46
]
to

\left\|u_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{H^{s+1}}\leq\epsilon_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}},\quad\left\|\left\langle\partial_{x}\right\rangle\theta_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{H^{s}}\leq\epsilon_{0}\nu^{\frac{2}{3}},

where
s>5
.

For
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{I}
with non-slip boundary data, Masmoudi–Zhai–Zhao
[
45
]
obtained the same threshold as (
1.6
). For
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{I}
with Navier-slip boundary data, the threshold was obtained by Wang–Yang
[
54
]
as follows:

\left\|u_{\text{in}}\right\|_{H^{4}}+\nu^{-\frac{7}{12}}\left\|\theta_{\text{in}}\right\|_{H^{3}}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\left\|\partial_{x}\theta_{\text{in}}\right\|_{H^{3}}\leq\epsilon_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}.

In the whole space
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
, Wang–Wang
[
52
]
proved the asymptotic stability of the steady state if the initial perturbations
(u_{\mathrm{in}},\theta_{\mathrm{in}})
satisfy

\left\|\omega_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{H^{1}\cap L^{1}}\leq\epsilon_{0}\nu^{\frac{3}{4}},\quad\left\|\theta_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{H^{1}\cap L^{1}}\leq\epsilon_{1}\nu^{\frac{5}{4}}.

Recently, for the Navier-Stokes equations on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
, Arbon–Bedrossian
[
1
]
proved

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq j\leq 1}\|\langle\partial_{x}\rangle^{m}\langle\frac{\partial_{x}}{\nu}\rangle^{-\frac{j}{3}}\partial_{y}^{j}w_{in}\|_{L^{2}_{x,y}}+\|w_{in,k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{k}L_{y}^{2}}\leq\delta_{1}\frac{\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+\ln(\frac{1}{\nu})^{1/2}},

where
m\in(1/2,1)
,
\langle\cdot\rangle:=\sqrt{1+(\cdot)^{2}}
and
\delta_{1}>0
is a constant.
Then, the result was improved in
[
38
]
by
Li–Liu–Zhao

\left\|\left\langle D_{x},D_{y}\right\rangle^{6}\left\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\right\rangle^{4}\omega_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|\left\langle D_{x},D_{y}\right\rangle^{5}\left\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\right\rangle^{4}\omega_{\mathrm{in}}\right\|_{L^{1}}\leq\nu^{\frac{1}{3}+\delta},

where
\delta>0
is small,
D=\frac{1}{i}\partial
and
\langle D_{x},D_{y}\rangle:=\sqrt{1+D_{x}^{2}+D_{y}^{2}}
.

For the 3D Boussinesq equations via the Couette flow, we refer to
[
17
,
18
,
23
]
and the reference therein.

Motivated by the progress in
[
38
]
, it is interesting whether the threshold holds for the Boussinesq system (
1.5
).

Our main result is follows:

Theorem 1.1
.

Suppose that
\nu=\mu
. For arbitrarily small
0<\delta<1
,
\frac{1-\delta}{2}<\epsilon<\frac{1}{2}
, there exists
0<c,\nu_{0}<1
such that for all
0<\nu\leq\nu_{0}
, if the initial data
\omega_{\mathrm{in}}
and
\theta_{\mathrm{in}}
satisfy

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\omega_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{L^{2}\cap L^{1}}\leq\nu^{\frac{1}{3}+\delta},

(1.7)

\displaystyle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\langle D_{x}\rangle\theta_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{L^{2}\cap L^{1}}\leq\nu^{\frac{2}{3}+2\delta},

then (
1.5
) has a pair of unique global solution
(\omega(t),\,\theta(t))
with the following stability estimate:

\displaystyle\|e^{c\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\lambda(D_{x})t}\langle D_{x}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\tfrac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{\epsilon}\omega\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,+\infty)}L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}-\delta}\|e^{c\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\lambda(D_{x})t}\langle D_{x}\rangle\langle\tfrac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{\epsilon}\langle D_{x}\rangle^{\frac{1}{3}}\theta\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,+\infty)}L^{2}}\leq C\nu^{\frac{1}{3}+\delta},

where
\lambda(k)=\min\{1,|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}\}
.

Remark 1.2
.

When the temperature
\theta
vanishes, the system reduces to the Navier-Stokes equations, as studied in Arbon–Bedrossian
[
1
]
and Li–Liu–Zhao
[
38
]
. The above theorem extends their results to the Boussinesq setting, achieving the same stability threshold. Compared with
[
38
]
, the main difficulty arises from the nonlinear term
u\cdot\nabla\theta
when certain derivatives are propagated onto
u
. In particular, the multiplier
\mathcal{M}_{3}
in
[
38
]
appears insufficient for estimating

|D_{x}|^{1/3}u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}}

(see Step VI in Proposition
5.1
). To address this, we construct
a new multiplier
\mathcal{M}_{3}
in (
2.1
), which is employed to handle the term
I_{4}
in Step VI:

\begin{gathered}I_{4}:=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\mathcal{M}(t,k,\xi)e^{2c\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\lambda(k)t}\langle k\rangle^{2}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{2\epsilon}|k|^{\frac{1}{3}}\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)|l|^{\frac{1}{3}}\frac{tl(k-l)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\xi-\eta)dkdld\xi d\eta\right|.\end{gathered}

When
|k-l|<|k|
, this new
\mathcal{M}_{3}
successfully absorbs the additional factor
|l|^{1/3}
due to the fact
\langle 1/l\rangle^{-2/3}{|l|^{-2/3}}\lesssim 1
.

Remark 1.3
.

It is worth mentioning that it remains challenging to improve the transition threshold of
\{\omega,\theta\}
from
\{\frac{1}{3}+\delta,\,\frac{2}{3}+2\delta\}
to
\{\frac{1}{3},\,\frac{2}{3}\}
under Sobolev perturbations. The latter seems to be the best known result for the Navier-Stokes equations in
\Omega=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
, as established by Masmoudi–Zhao
[
44
]
and Wei–Zhang
[
56
]
by different methods. The main difficulty lies in the singular behavior of the frequency variables
(l,\eta)
as
|l|+|\eta|\to 0
. For instance, when dealing with the term
u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}}
, it is difficult to use the inviscid damping to control
L^{2}
-norm of
u^{\mathrm{NL}}
. The additional
\delta
in the threshold, however, allows this estimate to succeed (see (
4.12
) in Lemma
4.2
for details).

Remark 1.4
.

For more precise estimates, we apply a standard quasi-linearization method to handle the difficulty arising from the buoyancy term
\partial_{x}\theta
. This approach is natural when making energy estimates
L^{p}
. The estimates of the equations for
\{\omega,\theta\}
are divided into linear and non-linear parts. Estimates for linear parts are determined by the size of the initial data and even the required regularity. For linear parts, there are many splendorous ideas introduced by Kelvin
[
34
]
for the incompressible Navier-Stokes equation in whole space. However, if it is coupled with the temperature equation, it seems puzzling. Here,
a new observation
is we calculate the Kelvin solution of the coupled system as in
[
28
]
and make some energy estimates in the sense of the Fourier transform, which will be used in the nonlinear parts.

Besides, the difficulty of the nonlinear term comes from the interaction between linear and nonlinear terms.
Inspired by the tricks in
[
56
]
, the calculations of the nonlinear parts will be separated into short time scale
t\leq\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}
and long time scale
t\geq\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}
. In the short time interval, we do not need to propagate the derivative of
\partial_{x}\theta
since the time integral can be handled. During the long time interval, we need to propagate some derivatives to deal with the buoyancy term. Due to the pointwise inviscid damping estimate of the potential function, i.e.
\Delta^{-1}
, it can provide a power of
t^{-1}
, which is effective to control some non-linear terms (see, e.g., (
5.2
) in Proposition
5.1
).

Remark 1.5
.

Another difficulty may lie in the estimates for Fourier frequency. For instance, when dealing with the nonlinear terms in the equation of
\omega
and
\theta
, we will encounter with the convolution for both horizontal and vertical frequency, it needs to carefully do some scaling and overcome the singularity. Inspired by
[
38
]
, one can follow their ideas when dealing with the transform between
k
,
l
and
k-l
. Moreover, the setting of
\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle
and
\langle D_{x}\rangle
can prevent the singularity in horizontal frequency in one hand, and share responsibility for integral index when using Young’s convolution inequality.

1.1.
The progress of Navier-Stokes equations

One special case of (
1
) is the Navier-Stokes equations by taking
\vartheta=0
. The stability of the Couette flow for the Navier-Stokes equation has been widely studied. Let us recall some related results.

When
\Omega=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
,

•

if the perturbation for vorticity is in the Gevrey class, then
\gamma=0

[
8
]
,

•

if the perturbation for velocity is in Sobolev space
H^{2}
, then
\gamma\leq\frac{1}{2}

[
9
]
,

•

if the perturbation for vorticity is in Sobolev space
H_{x}^{\text{log }}L_{y}^{2}
, then
\gamma\leq\frac{1}{2}

[
43
]
,

•

if the perturbation for vorticity is in Gevrey-
\frac{1}{s}
, then
\gamma\in\left[0,\frac{1}{3}\right]

[
39
]
,

•

if the perturbation for vorticity is in Sobolev space
H^{\sigma}(\sigma\geq 40)
, then
\gamma\leq\frac{1}{3}

[
44
]
,

•

if the perturbation for vorticity is in Sobolev space
H^{b}(b\geq 2)
, then
\gamma\leq\frac{1}{3}

[
56
]
.

When
\Omega=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{I}
,

•

if the velocity perturbation is in the Sobolev space
H^{2}
with non-slip boundary data, then
\gamma\leq\frac{1}{2}

[
13
]
,

•

if the perturbation for vorticity is in Sobolev space
H^{3}
with Navier-slip boundary data, then
\gamma\leq\frac{1}{3}

[
57
]
.

When
\Omega=\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
,

•

if the perturbation for velocity is in Sobolev space
L^{2}\cap L^{1}
, then
\gamma\leq\frac{3}{4}

[
53
]
,

•

the perturbation for velocity satisfies
[
1
]

\gamma\leq\frac{1}{2}+,

which was improved to
\gamma\leq\frac{1}{3}+
in

[
38
]
.
.

When
\Omega=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}
,

•

if the perturbation for velocity is in Gevery class, then
\gamma=1

[
5
,
6
]
,

•

if the perturbation for velocity is in Sobolev class
H^{\sigma}\left(\sigma>\frac{9}{2}\right)
, then
\gamma\leq\frac{3}{2}

[
3
]
,

•

if the perturbation for velocity is in Sobolev class
H^{2}
, then
\gamma\leq 1

[
55
]
.

When
\Omega=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{I}\times\mathbb{T}
,

•

if the velocity perturbation is in the Sobolev class
H^{2}
, then
\gamma\leq 1

[
15
]
.

As for the plane Poiseuille flow, the Kolmogorov flow or the rotation term, we refer to
[
20
,
25
,
26
,
14
,
12
,
29
,
40
]
and the reference therein.

1.2.
Two stability mechanisms

The study of the stability of Couette flow for the Navier-Stokes equation has been a prominent topic in fluid mechanics since the pioneering works of Kelvin
[
34
]
, Reyleigh
[
48
]
, Orr
[
47
]
and Sommerfeld
[
50
]
. Consider the vorticity formulation of the linearized Navier–Stokes equations via the Couette flow:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\omega+y\partial_{x}\omega-\nu\Delta\omega=0,\\
\left.\omega\right|_{t=0}=\omega_{\mathrm{in}}.\end{array}\right.

By Fourier transform, the Kelvin’s solution is

\hat{\omega}(t,k,\xi)=\hat{\omega}_{\mathrm{in}}(k,\xi+kt)e^{-\nu\int_{0}^{t}|k|^{2}+|\xi+k(t-s)|^{2}ds},

which satisfies the following two linear estimates:

\displaystyle|\hat{\omega}(t,k,\xi)|\leq C\left|\hat{\omega}_{\mathrm{in}}(k,\xi+kt)\right|e^{-c\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t},

(1.8)

\displaystyle|\hat{\phi}(t,k,\xi)|\leq C\langle t\rangle^{-2}\frac{1+|k|^{2}+|\xi+kt|^{2}}{|k|^{4}}\left|\hat{\omega}_{\text{in }}(k,\xi+kt)\right|e^{-c\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t},

(1.9)

for some
c>0
, where
\phi=\Delta^{-1}\omega
is the stream function.

1.2.1.
Enhanced dissipation

(
1.8
) is the enhanced dissipation estimate, since the dissipation time scale is
O\left(\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\right)
, which is much faster than the standard heat dissipation time scale
O\left(\nu^{-1}\right)
for small
\nu
. Clearly the dissipation rate is inhomogeneous and depends on the frequencies
k
.

The phenomenon of enhanced dissipation has been widely observed and studied in physics literature (see, e.g.,
[
11
,
37
,
49
,
51
]
). It has recently attracted enormous attention from the mathematics community and significant progress has been made. One of the earliest rigorous results on the enhanced dissipation is obtained by Constantin–Kiselev–Ryzhik–Zlatos
[
16
]
on the enhancement of diffusive mixing. However, the quantitative enhanced dissipation rate is usually hard to obtain except for some special flows such as shear flow, spiral flow and Anosov flow
[
19
]
.

In additional to the transition threshold problem, the enhanced dissipation also plays an important role for the suppression of blow-up in the Keller-Segel system
[
2
,
35
]
. Let us refer to
[
22
,
24
]
for more recent works.

1.2.2.
Inviscid damping

(
1.9
) is so called inviscid damping estimate, which is due to the mixing of the vorticity
induced by the shear flow. This effect is mainly based on the following inequality

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{2}}{|l|^{4}\langle t\rangle^{2}}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{4}\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{2},

which plays an important role in our proof.

Inviscid damping is analogous to Landau damping in plasma physics found by Landau
[
36
]
. For general shear flows, establishing linear inviscid damping remains a challenging problem. In a series of works
[
58
,
59
,
60
]
, Wei-Zhang-Zhao proved linear inviscid damping for both monotone and certain non-monotone flows, including the Poiseuille and Kolmogorov flows. We refer the reader to
[
47
,
10
,
21
,
33
,
61
,
62
]
and the references therein for related results and recent developments on linear inviscid damping. Nonlinear inviscid damping poses even greater difficulties. Inspired by the groundbreaking work of Mouhot-Villani
[
41
]
on nonlinear Landau damping, Bedrossian-Masmoudi
[
7
]
established nonlinear inviscid damping for the Couette flow in the domain
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
. We refer to
[
30
,
31
,
32
,
27
,
42
]
for recent significant progress on nonlinear inviscid damping.

1.3.
Some notations and outlines

Here are some notations used in this paper.

Notations
:

•

For a given function
f(x,y)
on on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, its
k
-th horizontal Fourier modes can be defined by

f_{k}(y)=\mathcal{F}_{x\rightarrow k}(f)(k,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(x,y)\mathrm{e}^{-ikx}dx,

In addition, we denote

\hat{f}_{k}(\xi)=\hat{f}(k,\xi)=\mathcal{F}_{y\rightarrow\xi}(f_{k})(y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}f(x,y)\mathrm{e}^{-i(kx+\xi y)}dxdy.

•

Denote
C
by a positive constant independent of
\nu
,
\mu
,
t
and the initial data, and it may be different from line to line.
A\lesssim B
means there exists a absolutely constant
C
, such that
A\leq CB
.

•

Denote by
(f|g)
the
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)
inner product of
f
and
g
.

•

The space norm
\|f\|_{L^{p}}
is defined by
\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|f|^{p}dxdy\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}
. The time-space norm
\|f\|_{L_{T}^{q}L^{p}}
is defined by
\|f\|_{L_{T}^{q}L^{p}}=\|\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\ \|_{L^{q}(0,T)}
.
Moreover,
\|f\|_{L_{(T_{1},T_{2})}^{q}L^{p}}=\|\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\ \|_{L^{q}(T_{1},T_{2})}
. For simplicity, we write
\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
as
\|f\|_{L^{p}}.

The paper is organized as follows. Section
2
presents the main ideas and proof of the Theorem
1.1
. Section
3
is dedicated to deriving estimates for the linear part. In Section
4
, we establish the energy estimates in short time scale
t\leq T_{0}=\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}
. While in Section
5
, we establish the energy estimates in long time scale
t\geq T_{0}=\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}
.

2.
The proof of the main theorem

2.1.
Construction of the multipliers

For
k,\xi\in\mathbb{R}
and small
\nu,\kappa>0
, denote that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{1}(k,\xi)

\displaystyle:=\arctan\left(\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{-\frac{1}{3}}\operatorname{sgn}(k)\xi\right)+\frac{\pi}{2},

(2.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{2}(k,\xi)

\displaystyle:=\arctan\left(\frac{\xi}{k}\right)+\frac{\pi}{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{3}(t,k,\xi)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{-\frac{1}{3}-\kappa}\frac{1}{|l|^{\frac{4}{3}}}\left(\operatorname{sgn}(l)\arctan\left(\frac{\xi+t(k-l)}{1+|k-l|+|l|}\right)+\frac{\pi}{2}\right)dl.

Then the three multipliers above are self-adjoint Fourier multipliers and verify that

1\leq\mathcal{M}:=\mathcal{M}_{1}+\mathcal{M}_{2}+\mathcal{M}_{3}+1\leq C_{\kappa}.

(2.2)

We also denote

\displaystyle\Upsilon(t,k,\xi):=\left(-\partial_{t}+k\partial_{\xi}\right)\mathcal{M}_{3}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{-\frac{1}{3}-\kappa}\frac{1}{|l|^{\frac{1}{3}}}\frac{1+|k-l|+|l|}{(1+|k-l|+|l|)^{2}+|\xi+t(k-l)|^{2}}dl,

(2.3)

with
\Upsilon(t,k,\xi)>0
for all
t>0
and
k,\xi\in\mathbb{R}
.

The enhanced dissipation multiplier
\mathcal{M}_{1}
and the inviscid damping multiplier
\mathcal{M}_{2}
are motivated by
[
28
]
. The construction of multiplier
\mathcal{M}_{3}
is motivated by
[
56
]
. The multiplier
\mathcal{M}_{3}
is designed to control the growth of the reaction term caused by echo cascades.

An important observation
is that the multiplier
\mathcal{M}_{3}
constructed here is not only effective in controlling the real reaction terms
u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\omega^{\mathrm{L}}
and
u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla|D_{x}|^{1/3}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}
, but also sufficient to handle staggered derivative term
|D_{x}|^{1/3}u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}}
(see around (
5.23
) and (
5.30
) for more details).
On the Fourier side, these multipliers can be understood as some kind of ‘ghost weight’, which are bounded weights providing additional dissipation properties. The most crucial feature of the three multipliers mentioned above is that

2\Re\left(\left(\partial_{t}+y\partial_{x}\right)f\mid\mathcal{M}_{i}f\right)=\frac{d}{dt}\|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{i}}f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(-\partial_{t}+k\partial_{\xi}\right)\mathcal{M}_{i}(t,k,\xi)|\hat{f}|^{2}dkd\xi,

for some
f=f(t,x,y)
on
(0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Lemma 2.1
.

For smooth enough function
f=f(t,x,y)
on
(0,+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left(-\partial_{t}+k\partial_{\xi}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(t,k,\xi)|\hat{f}|^{2}dkd\xi

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}}{4}\|\left|D_{x}\right|^{\frac{1}{3}}f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\frac{\nu}{2}\|\partial_{y}f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}\nabla\Delta^{-1}f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\Upsilon}f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(2.4)

Proof.

Direct calculation shows that

k\partial_{\xi}\mathcal{M}_{1}(k,\xi)=\frac{\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}}{1+\nu^{\frac{2}{3}}|k|^{-\frac{2}{3}}|\xi|^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{4}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}-\frac{1}{2}\nu|\xi|^{2}

and

k\partial_{\xi}\mathcal{M}_{2}(k,\xi)=\frac{k^{2}}{k^{2}+\xi^{2}},

which, along with (
2.3
), completes the proof.
∎

2.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

The proof of Theorem
1.1
relies on the following three key propositions, which will be proved in next sections:

Proposition 2.2
.

For initial data satisfying (
1.7
), there exists a universal constant
0<c_{0}<\frac{1}{16\pi}
such that for any
t>0
, there holds that

\displaystyle\|e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\left|D_{x}\right|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}

\displaystyle\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\nu^{\frac{1}{6}}\|e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\left|D_{x}\right|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\left|D_{x}\right|^{\frac{1}{3}}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\nu^{\frac{1}{3}+\delta}

and

\displaystyle\|e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\left|D_{x}\right|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}

\displaystyle\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\langle D_{x}\rangle\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\nu^{\frac{1}{6}}\|e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\left|D_{x}\right|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\left|D_{x}\right|^{\frac{1}{3}}\langle D_{x}\rangle\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\lesssim\nu^{\frac{2}{3}+2\delta}.

Proposition 2.3
.

For initial data satisfying (
1.7
),
T_{0}=\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}
and
1<p<2
, there exists a small enough
0<\nu_{1}<1
such that if
0<\nu<\nu_{1}
, it holds that

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{\infty}L^{p}}\lesssim\nu^{\frac{2}{3}+2\delta},

(2.5)

\displaystyle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{\infty}L^{p}}\lesssim\nu^{1+3\delta},

\displaystyle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{\infty}L^{p}}\lesssim\nu^{\frac{5}{6}+3\delta}.

Corollary 2.4
.

Under the same assumptions as in Proposition
2.3
, there exists a small enough
0<\nu_{1}<1
such that if
0<\nu<\nu_{1}
, there holds that

\displaystyle\|e^{c\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\lambda\left(D_{x}\right)t}\left\langle D_{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\right\rangle^{\epsilon}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{\infty}L^{2}}\lesssim\nu^{\frac{2}{3}+2\delta},

\displaystyle\|e^{c\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\lambda\left(D_{x}\right)t}\left\langle D_{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\right\rangle^{\epsilon}\left\langle D_{x}\right\rangle^{\frac{1}{3}}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{\infty}L^{2}}\lesssim\nu^{\frac{5}{6}+3\delta}.

Proof.

For some
f
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|e^{c\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\lambda\left(D_{x}\right)t}\left\langle D_{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\right\rangle^{\epsilon}f\right\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\left\langle D_{x}\right\rangle\left\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\right\rangle^{\epsilon}f\right\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{\infty}L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{2}\hat{f}_{k}(\xi)\right\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{\infty}L_{k,\xi}^{p^{\prime}}}\left\|\left\langle\frac{1}{k}\right\rangle^{\epsilon}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{-1}\right\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{\infty}L_{k,\xi}^{\frac{2p}{2-p}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\left\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\right\rangle^{2}f\right\|_{L_{T_{0}}^{\infty}L^{p}},

where we used that for small
\epsilon<\frac{1}{2}
, if we choose
1<p<2
sufficiently close to
1
such that
\frac{1}{p}>\epsilon+\frac{1}{2}
, then we have

\left\|\left\langle\frac{1}{k}\right\rangle^{\epsilon}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{-1}\right\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{\frac{2p}{2-p}}}<\infty.

Then, Proposition
2.3
completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 2.5
.

For initial data satisfying (
1.7
),
T_{0}=\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}
, there exists a small enough
0<\nu_{2}<1
such that if
0<\nu<\nu_{2}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|e^{c\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\lambda(D_{x})t}\langle D_{x}\rangle\langle\tfrac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{\epsilon}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[T_{0},+\infty]}L^{2}}+\|e^{c\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\lambda\left(D_{x}\right)t}\langle D_{x}\rangle\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}\nabla\phi^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L_{[T_{0},+\infty]}^{2}L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}-\delta}\|e^{c\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\lambda(D_{x})t}\langle D_{x}\rangle\langle\tfrac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{\epsilon}\langle D_{x}\rangle^{\frac{1}{3}}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[T_{0},+\infty]}L^{2}}\lesssim\nu^{\frac{1}{2}+2\delta}.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Denoting
\nu_{0}=\min\{\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\}
and using Proposition
2.2
, Corollary
2.4
and Proposition
2.5
, the proof is complete.
∎

3.
Estimates of the linear part

Denote
\omega^{\mathrm{L}}
and
\theta^{\mathrm{L}}
by a pair of solutions that solve the following linear equation:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}-\nu\Delta\omega^{\mathrm{L}}+y\partial_{x}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}=\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{L}},\\
\partial_{t}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}-\mu\Delta\theta^{\mathrm{L}}+y\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}=0,\\
\left.\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\right|_{t=0}=\omega_{\text{in}}(x,y),\quad\left.\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\right|_{t=0}=\theta_{\text{in}}(x,y).\end{array}\right.

It is easy to check that the operator
\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{a}
,
a\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, commutes with the differential operator with variable coefficients
\partial_{t}+y\partial_{x}
and thus

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\partial_{t}-\nu\Delta+y\partial_{x})\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{a}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}=\partial_{x}\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{a}\theta^{\mathrm{L}},\\
(\partial_{t}-\mu\Delta+y\partial_{x})\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{a}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}=0,\\
\left.\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{a}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\right|_{t=0}=\langle D_{x},D_{y}\rangle^{a}\omega_{\text{in}}(x,y),\\
\left.\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{a}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\right|_{t=0}=\langle D_{x},D_{y}\rangle^{a}\theta_{\text{in}}(x,y).\end{array}\right.

The solution to the equations above can be represented by the Fourier methods:

\displaystyle\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{a}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}(t,k,\xi)

\displaystyle=\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{a}\widehat{\theta_{\text{in}}}(k,\xi+kt)e^{-\mu\int_{0}^{t}|k|^{2}+|\xi+k(t-\tau)|^{2}d\tau},

(3.4)

\displaystyle\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{a}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}(t,k,\xi)

\displaystyle=\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{a}\widehat{\omega_{\text{in}}}(k,\xi+kt)e^{-\nu\int_{0}^{t}|k|^{2}+|\xi+k(t-\tau)|^{2}d\tau}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}ik\langle k,\xi+ks\rangle^{a}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}(s,k,\xi)e^{-\nu\int_{0}^{t-s}|k|^{2}+|\xi+k(t-\tau)|^{2}d\tau}ds.

When
\nu=\mu
, substituting
\eqref{eq:solutions of the linear equ}_{1}
into
\eqref{eq:solutions of the linear equ}_{2}
yields that

\displaystyle\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{a}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}(t,k,\xi)

\displaystyle=\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{a}\widehat{\omega_{\text{in}}}(k,\xi+kt)e^{-\nu\int_{0}^{t}|k|^{2}+|\xi+k(t-\tau)|^{2}d\tau}

(3.5)

\displaystyle+e^{-\nu\int_{0}^{t}|k|^{2}+|\xi+k(t-\tau)|^{2}d\tau}\int_{0}^{t}ik\langle k,\xi+ks\rangle^{a}\widehat{\theta_{\text{in}}}(k,\xi+ks)ds.

With the solution above, we can obtain estimates at each time instant in the frequency space.

Lemma 3.1
.

For initial data satisfying (
1.7
), there exists a universal constant
0<c_{0}<\frac{1}{12}
such that for
2\leq p\leq\infty
and for any
t>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|e^{c_{0}\nu|k|^{2}t^{3}}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\langle k\rangle\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}(t)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{p}}\leq\nu^{\frac{2}{3}+2\delta},

\displaystyle\|e^{c_{0}\nu|k|^{2}t^{3}}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}(t)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{p}}\leq C(c_{0})\nu^{\frac{1}{3}+\delta}.

Proof.

For
2\leq p\leq\infty
,
t\geq 0
and
c_{0}<\frac{1}{12}
, we deduce from
\eqref{eq:solutions of the linear equ}_{1}
and (
1.7
) that

\displaystyle\|e^{c_{0}\nu|k|^{2}t^{3}}

\displaystyle\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\langle k\rangle\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}(t,k,\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\langle k\rangle\widehat{\theta_{\text{in}}}(k,\xi+kt)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{p}}=\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\langle k\rangle\widehat{\theta_{\text{in}}}(k,\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\langle k\rangle\widehat{\theta_{\text{in}}}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2}{p}}\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\langle k\rangle\widehat{\theta_{\text{in}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1-\frac{2}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\langle D_{x}\rangle\theta_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2}{p}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\langle D_{x}\rangle\theta_{\mathrm{in}}\|_{L^{1}}^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\leq\nu^{\frac{2}{3}+2\delta},

where we used that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|k|^{2}+|\xi+k(t-\tau)|^{2}d\tau=|k|^{2}t+\frac{|k|^{2}t^{3}}{12}+\left|\xi+\frac{kt}{2}\right|^{2}t.

Similarly, by (
3.5
) and (
1.7
) again, we have

\displaystyle\|e^{c_{0}\nu|k|^{2}t^{3}}

\displaystyle\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}(t,k,\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\omega_{\text{in}}}(k,\xi+kt)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{p}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\|e^{(c_{0}-\frac{1}{12})\nu|k|^{2}t^{3}}\int_{0}^{t}ik\langle k,\xi+ks\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\theta_{\text{in}}}(k,\xi+ks)ds\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{p}}

\displaystyle=\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\omega_{\text{in}}}(k,\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{p}}+\|e^{(c_{0}-\frac{1}{12})\nu|k|^{2}t^{3}}ikt\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\theta_{\text{in}}}(k,\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\omega_{\text{in}}}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2}{p}}\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\omega_{\text{in}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1-\frac{2}{p}}

\displaystyle\qquad+(\frac{1}{12}-c_{0})^{-\frac{1}{3}}\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}|k|^{\frac{1}{3}}\widehat{\theta_{\text{in}}}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2}{p}}\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}|k|^{\frac{1}{3}}\widehat{\theta_{\text{in}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1-\frac{2}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C(c_{0})\nu^{\frac{1}{3}+\delta},

where we used

\displaystyle|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}te^{(c_{0}-\frac{1}{12})\nu|k|^{2}t^{3}}\leq(\frac{1}{12}-c_{0})^{-\frac{1}{3}}\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}.

The proof is completed.
∎

Analogously, in the frequency space, one can also obtain the following lemma by using the Fourier multiplier
\mathcal{M}_{1}
.

Lemma 3.2
.

For initial data satisfying (
1.7
) and any
m>0
, there exists a universal constant
0<c_{0}<\frac{m^{p-1}}{8(m+\pi)^{p}}
such that for
1\leq p<+\infty
and for any
t>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\langle k,

\displaystyle\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\langle|k|^{\frac{1}{3}}\rangle\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2p}}

\displaystyle+\nu^{\frac{1}{6p}}\|e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\langle|k|^{\frac{1}{3}}\rangle|k|^{\frac{1}{3p}}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L_{t}^{2p}L^{2p}}\lesssim\nu^{\frac{2}{3}+2\delta}

and

\displaystyle\|e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\langle k,

\displaystyle\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2p}}

\displaystyle+\nu^{\frac{1}{6p}}\|e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}|k|^{\frac{1}{3p}}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L_{t}^{2p}L^{2p}}\lesssim\nu^{\frac{1}{3}+\delta}.

Proof.

Estimates of
\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}
.

Denoting that
\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi):={\mathcal{M}_{1}}(k,\xi)+m
and
M:=m+\pi
, it holds that
m<\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)<M
. Applying the Fourier transform for
\eqref{eq:linear equ}_{2}
, one obtains

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}-k\partial_{\xi})\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}(t,k,\xi)+\mu(k^{2}+\xi^{2})\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}(t,k,\xi)=0.

(3.6)

Thanks to

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\right)

\displaystyle=c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}

\displaystyle\quad+(2p-1)\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}

\displaystyle\qquad\times|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\partial_{t}(\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}),

\displaystyle\partial_{\xi}\left(|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\right)

\displaystyle=p\partial_{\xi}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi,\mu)|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}

\displaystyle\quad+(2p-1)\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\partial_{\xi}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}},

and taking the inner product of
(
3.6
)
with

2p|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}},

we have

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big|2p|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\big(\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big|2p|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi,\mu)e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-2p\big(\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big|\partial_{t}(|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}})\big)

\displaystyle=2p\frac{d}{dt}\||\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-2pc_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\||k|^{\frac{1}{3}}|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-(2p-1)\frac{d}{dt}\||\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\||\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-2pc_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\||k|^{\frac{1}{3}}|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

\displaystyle\big(k\partial_{\xi}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big|

\displaystyle 2p|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big)

\displaystyle=-2p\big(k\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big|\partial_{\xi}(|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}})\big)

\displaystyle=-2p^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k\partial_{\xi}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}|^{2p-2}|e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p}dkd\xi

\displaystyle\quad-2p(2p-1)\langle k\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}},\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\partial_{\xi}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\rangle

and

\displaystyle\big(\mu(k^{2}+\xi^{2})\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big|2p|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big)

\displaystyle=2p\mu\|(k^{2}+\xi^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

which imply

\displaystyle-\big(k\partial_{\xi}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big|

\displaystyle 2p|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)e^{2c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big)

\displaystyle=p\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k\partial_{\xi}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}|^{2p-2}|e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p}dkd\xi

\displaystyle\geq\frac{pm^{p-1}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}}{4}\||k|^{\frac{1}{3}}|e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\frac{pM^{p-1}\mu}{2}\||\xi||e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

and

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\||\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-2pc_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\||k|^{\frac{1}{3}}|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+2p\mu\|(k^{2}+\xi^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+p\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k\partial_{\xi}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}|^{2p-2}|e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p}dkd\xi=0.

Since
m<\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi,\mu)<M
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\||\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{p\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}}{4}(m^{p-1}-8c_{0}M^{p})\||k|^{\frac{1}{3}}|e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+pm^{p}\mu\||k||e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\mu p}{2}(2m^{p}-M^{p-1})\||\xi||e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{p}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq 0.

Then, thanks to
c_{0}<\frac{m^{p-1}}{8M^{p}}=\frac{m^{p-1}}{8(m+\pi)^{p}}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L^{2p}}^{2p}+\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\||k|^{\frac{1}{3p}}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L^{2p}}^{2p}\leq 0.

(3.7)

Since the operator
\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}
commutes with the differential operator with variable coefficients
\partial_{t}+y\partial_{x}
, we get that

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\langle|k|^{\frac{1}{3}}\rangle\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2p}}^{2p}

(3.8)

\displaystyle+\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\langle|k|^{\frac{1}{3}}\rangle|k|^{\frac{1}{3p}}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L^{2p}L^{2p}}^{2p}\lesssim\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\langle|k|^{\frac{1}{3}}\rangle\widehat{\theta_{\rm in}}\|_{L^{2p}}^{2p}.

Estimates of
\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}
.

Applying Fourier transform for
\eqref{eq:linear equ}_{1}
, we get that

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}-k\partial_{\xi})\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}(t,k,\xi)+\nu(k^{2}+\xi^{2})\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}(t,k,\xi)=ik\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}.

Note that (when
\nu=\mu
)

\displaystyle\big(ik\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big|2p|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)e^{2c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(|k|^{\frac{p+1}{3p}}\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\big||k|^{\frac{2p-1}{3p}}|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}|^{2p-2}\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\langle|k|^{\frac{1}{3}}\rangle|k|^{\frac{1}{3p}}\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L^{2p}}\||k|^{\frac{1}{3p}}\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L^{2p}}^{2p-1}.

Similarly, as (
3.7
) we get

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\sqrt{\mathcal{M}_{1}^{\prime}(k,\xi)}e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L^{2p}}^{2p}+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\||k|^{\frac{1}{3p}}e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L^{2p}}^{2p}\lesssim\nu^{-\frac{2p-1}{3}}\|\langle|k|^{\frac{1}{3}}\rangle|k|^{\frac{1}{3p}}e^{c_{0}\mu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\widehat{\theta^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L^{2p}}^{2p},

provided with
c_{0}<\frac{m^{p-1}}{8(m+\pi)^{p}}
. Integrating the above inequality and combining with (
3.8
), one has

\displaystyle\|e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}

\displaystyle\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2p}}^{2p}+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|e^{c_{0}\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}t}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}|k|^{\frac{1}{3p}}\widehat{\omega^{\mathrm{L}}}\|_{L^{2p}L^{2p}}^{2p}

(3.9)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\omega_{\rm in}}\|_{L^{2p}}^{2p}+\nu^{-\frac{2p-1}{3}}\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\langle|k|^{\frac{1}{3}}\rangle\widehat{\theta_{\rm in}}\|_{L^{2p}}^{2p}.

By the interpolation inequality and the Hausdorff-Young inequality, it holds

\displaystyle\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\omega_{\rm in}}\|_{L^{2p}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\omega_{\rm in}}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\widehat{\omega_{\rm in}}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}

(3.10)

\displaystyle\leq\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\omega_{\rm in}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{p}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\omega_{\rm in}\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\leq\nu^{\frac{1}{3}+\delta}

and similarly

\displaystyle\|\langle k,\xi\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{k}\rangle^{4}\langle|k|^{\frac{1}{3}}\rangle\widehat{\theta_{\rm in}}\|_{L^{2p}}\leq\nu^{\frac{2}{3}+2\delta}.

(3.11)

Combining (
3.8
), (
3.9
), (
3.10
) and (
3.11
), we complete the proof.
∎

Remark 3.3
.

In Lemma
3.2
, we cannot let
p\to\infty
, as this would cause the constant
c
to vanish for any fixed
m>0
. This issue arises from the limitation in the construction of the multiplier
\mathcal{M}_{1}
. However, in the subsequent analysis, only the case
p=1
is required. In this setting, we choose
m=\pi
, which yields the following Proposition
2.2
.

Proof of Proposition
2.2
.

The proof is similar to Lemma
3.2
and we omit it.
∎

4.
Energy estimates in short time scale
t\leq T_{0}=\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}

At the beginning of this section, due to the lack of reverse Hausdorff–Young inequalities, we provide the following estimate for the linear part.

Lemma 4.1
.

For
t\leq T_{0}=\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}
and
1<p<2
, if the initial data
(\omega_{\mathrm{in}},\theta_{\mathrm{in}})
satisfies
(\ref{eq:the initial data})
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{p}}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}-\delta}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\langle D_{x}\rangle\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{p}}\lesssim\nu^{\frac{1}{3}+\delta}.

Proof.

Applying
\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\langle D_{x}\rangle
to
\eqref{eq:linear equ}_{2}
and taking the inner product of the resulting equation with
\left|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\langle D_{x}\rangle\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\right|^{p-2}\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\langle D_{x}\rangle\theta^{\mathrm{L}}
, we find that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\langle D_{x}\rangle\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{p}}\leq 0,

which implies

\displaystyle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\langle D_{x}\rangle\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{p}}\leq\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}\rangle^{2}\langle D_{x}\rangle\theta_{in}\|_{L^{p}}\leq\nu^{\frac{2}{3}+2\delta}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\langle D_{x}\rangle\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{p}},

from which, we deduce for
t\leq\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}
that

\displaystyle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{p}}\lesssim\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}\rangle^{2}\omega_{in}\|_{L^{p}}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{2}\langle D_{x}\rangle\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{p}}\lesssim\nu^{\frac{1}{3}+\delta}.

∎

Denote that
\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}=\omega-\omega^{\mathrm{L}}
and
\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}=\theta-\theta^{\mathrm{L}}
, which solve:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}-\nu\Delta\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}+y\partial_{x}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}+\left(u^{\mathrm{L}}+u^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\cdot\nabla\left(\omega^{\mathrm{L}}+\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)=\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}},\\
\partial_{t}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}-\nu\Delta\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}+y\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}+\left(u^{\mathrm{L}}+u^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\cdot\nabla\left(\theta^{\mathrm{L}}+\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)=0,\\
u^{\mathrm{L}}=\left(-\partial_{y},\partial_{x}\right)(-\Delta)^{-1}\omega^{\mathrm{L}},\quad u^{\mathrm{NL}}=\left(-\partial_{y},\partial_{x}\right)(-\Delta)^{-1}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}},\\
\left.\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\right|_{t=0}=0,\quad\left.\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right|_{t=0}=0.\end{array}\right.

Note that, in short time scale
t\leq T_{0}=\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}
, the Fourier multipliers is not necessary for the energy estimates, since the upper bound of
t
is sufficient for our stability threshold, i.e.,

\displaystyle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\lesssim\ \nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\text{``other terms''.}

Next we prove it in details.

4.1.
L^{2}
-estimate.

Lemma 4.2
.

For
1<p<2
, it holds

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left[\langle t\rangle^{-2}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad+\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}\left(\|\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{p}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad+\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\quad\times\left(\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\right].

Proof.

Taking the inner product of
\eqref{eq:nonlinear part}_{2}
with
\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}
, we get that

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(4.5)

\displaystyle=-\Re\big(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\left[(u^{\mathrm{L}}+u^{\mathrm{NL}})\cdot\nabla(\theta^{\mathrm{L}}+\theta^{\mathrm{NL}})\right]\big|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\big).

Estimate of
u^{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}}
.

First, for the source term
u^{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}}
, we write by the Fourier transform that

\displaystyle\left|\big(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}(u^{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}})\big|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\big)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\eta)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\xi-\eta)dkdld\xi d\eta\right|.

Using

\displaystyle\lvert\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)\rvert

\displaystyle=\lvert(\eta+lt)(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta+(k-l)t)\rvert

(4.6)

\displaystyle\leq\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle,

the point-wise inviscid damping estimate

\frac{1}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{2}}{|l|^{4}\langle t\rangle^{2}}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{4}\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{2},

(4.7)

and

\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}\lesssim\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{3}\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}

(4.8)

one has

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left|\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\left(u^{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\right)\mid\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right|

(4.9)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2}\|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{2}}\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{4}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\eta)\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{-2}\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{6}\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}.

Estimate of
u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}}
.

Applying the Fourier transform to the reaction term
u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}(u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}})\big|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\xi-\eta)dkdld\xi d\eta\right|.

Then, we divide the estimate into two cases.

\bullet
Case 1:
|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}\geq 1
. In this case, one can use (
4.8
) and

\displaystyle|k-l|+|\xi-\eta|\leq|k-l|+|\xi-\eta+(k-l)t|+|k-l|t\lesssim\langle t\rangle\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle

(4.10)

to prove

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}\geq 1}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\xi-\eta)dkdld\xi d\eta\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{2}}\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{3}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}\langle t\rangle\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{-2}\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{6}\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}.

\bullet
Case 2:
|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}<1
. To overcome the singularity near
\{l,\eta\}=\{0,0\}
, we make use of

\displaystyle|l|+|\eta+lt|\leq\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{1-\delta}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}(|l|+|\eta|)^{\delta}

(4.11)

to rewrite (
4.8
) as

\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}\lesssim\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}+\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{3-\delta}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}(|l|+|\eta|)^{\delta}

which, combined with (
4.10
), implies

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}<1}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\xi-\eta)dkdld\xi d\eta\right|

(4.12)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle\|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{2}}\left[\|\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{p^{\prime}}}\|(|l|+|\eta|)^{-1}\left.\chi\right|_{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}<1}\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{p}}\right.

\displaystyle\times\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{4}\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}+\langle t\rangle^{\delta}\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{3-\delta}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle\left.\times\|(|l|+|\eta|)^{\delta-1}\left.\chi\right|_{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}<1}\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{4}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\|\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{p}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right).

Combining the two cases above, we arrive at

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left|\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\left(u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\right)\mid\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right|

(4.13)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\|\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{p}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right).

Estimate of
u\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}
.
For this term, we employ the classical commutator estimate:

\displaystyle|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}-\langle k-l,

\displaystyle\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(|l|+|\eta+lt|)\left(\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{2}+\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{2}\right).

(4.14)

Thanks to

\left(u\cdot\nabla\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\big|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)=0,

we get that

\displaystyle\left|\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}(u\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}})\big|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\left(\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}-\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}\hat{\omega}_{l}(\eta)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)dkdld\xi d\eta\right|.

We then divide the estimate into two cases as above.

\bullet
Case 1:
|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}\geq 1
.
By (
4.10
) and (
4.1
), it holds that

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|l|+|\eta|\geq 1}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\left(\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}-\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}\right)\right.

(4.15)

\displaystyle\left.\quad\times\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}\hat{\omega}_{l}(\eta)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)dkdld\xi d\eta\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\|_{L_{k,t}^{2}}\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{3}\hat{\omega}_{l}(\eta)\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{-2}\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}+\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{-2}\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\langle t\rangle\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega\|_{L^{2}}.

\bullet
Case 2:
|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}<1
. Similarly, to overcome the singularity, we put (
4.1
) and (
4.11
) together to obtain that

\displaystyle|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}-

\displaystyle\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{1-\delta}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}(|l|+|\eta|)^{\delta}\left(\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{2}+\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{2}\right),

which along with (
4.10
) shows that,

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left|\int_{|l|+|\eta|<1}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\left(\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}-\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}\right)\right.

(4.16)

\displaystyle\left.\quad\times\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}\hat{\omega}_{l}(\eta)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)dkdld\xi d\eta\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{2}}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}\|(|l|+|\eta|)^{\delta-1}\left.\chi\right|_{|l|+|\eta|<1}\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}\left(\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{3-\delta}\hat{\omega}_{l}(\eta)\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\times\langle t\rangle\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}+\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{1-\delta}\hat{\omega}_{l}(\eta)\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\langle t\rangle\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega\|_{L^{2}}.

The combination of (
4.15
) and (
4.16
) yields

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left|\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\left(u\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\mid\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right|

(4.17)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega\|_{L^{2}}.

Summing up (
4.5
), (
4.9
), (
4.13
) and (
4.17
), we finish the proof.
∎

For
\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}
, a similar argument as in the above lemma yields the following.

Lemma 4.3
.

For
1<p<2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}(\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\times(\|\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{p}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}).

For
\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}
, the most troublesome term is
\partial_{x}u\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}
, due to the presence of an additional derivative falling on
u
and the lack of derivative on
\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}
. However, for the short-time scale estimate, it suffices to work with

\nu^{1/2}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}.

Lemma 4.4
.

For
1<p<2
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\nabla\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\bigg[\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{4}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\langle t\rangle^{-2}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{4}\langle\partial_{x}\rangle\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}+\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}+\langle t\rangle^{-1}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{4}\langle\partial_{x}\rangle\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{p}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad+\langle t\rangle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\bigg].

Proof.

Taking the inner product of
\eqref{eq:nonlinear part}_{2}
with
\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\partial_{x}^{2}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}
gives that

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\nu\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\nabla\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(4.18)

\displaystyle=-\Re\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\left[\left(u^{\mathrm{L}}+u^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\cdot\nabla\left(\theta^{\mathrm{L}}+\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right]\mid\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right).

Estimate of
u\cdot\nabla\partial_{x}\theta
.
Adapting the argument in Lemma
4.2
, we derive

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left|\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}(u\cdot\nabla\partial_{x}\theta)\big|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right|

(4.19)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left[\langle t\rangle^{-2}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad+\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}\left(\|\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{p}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad+\langle t\rangle^{1+\delta}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\quad\times\left(\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\right].

Estimate of
\partial_{x}u^{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}}
.
Thanks to the
\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle
derivative in the initial data, the additional
\partial_{x}
derivative on
u
can be absorbed.
Using

\frac{|l|}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{2}}{|l|^{3}\langle t\rangle^{2}}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{3}\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{2},

we get similarly as (
4.9
) that

\displaystyle\left|\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}(\partial_{x}u^{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}})\big|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{D_{x}}\rangle^{4}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}.

(4.20)

Estimate of
\partial_{x}u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}}
.
For
\partial_{x}u^{\mathrm{NL}}
, the classical Riesz transform theory allows us to bypass any concern about singularities (see (
4.21
)).
Writing

\displaystyle\left|\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}(\partial_{x}u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}})\big|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}k\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}l\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\xi-\eta)dkdld\xi d\eta\right|

and using (
4.8
), (
4.10
) and

\frac{|l|(|\eta|+|l|)}{l^{2}+\eta^{2}}\lesssim 1,

(4.21)

one gets

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left|\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}(\partial_{x}u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{L}})\big|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right|

(4.22)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}k\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{2}}\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{3}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}\langle t\rangle\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{-2}\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{6}\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\theta^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}.

Estimate of
\partial_{x}u^{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}
.
This term is challenging, since one cannot apply commutator estimates and is forced to work with the less favorable term
\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}
.
Using

\displaystyle\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}\lesssim\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}+\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{3}

we have

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left|\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}(\partial_{x}u^{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}})\big|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right|

(4.23)

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}k\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}l\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\eta)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)dkdld\xi d\eta\right|.

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}\left|k\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}l\right|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{3}\left|\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\eta)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)\right|dkdld\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}\left|k\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}l\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\eta)\right|

\displaystyle\times\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}\left|\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)\right|dkdld\xi d\eta=:I_{k-l}+I_{l}.

For
I_{k-l}
, by (
4.10
) and

\displaystyle\frac{|l|(|l|+|\eta|)}{l^{2}+\eta^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle}{(|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{2}}{|l|^{2}\langle t\rangle}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-1}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{2}\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{2},

(4.24)

we get that

\displaystyle I_{k-l}^{1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}k\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{2}}\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{5}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{2}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\eta)\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{-2}\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}

(4.25)

\displaystyle\times\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{4}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}.

For
I_{l}
, by (
4.24
), we infer that

\displaystyle I_{l}^{1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}k\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{2}}\langle t\rangle^{-1}\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{4}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{2}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{L}}(\eta)\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{-2}\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}

(4.26)

\displaystyle\times\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}\left(|k-l|+|\xi-\eta|\right)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\langle t\rangle^{-1}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{4}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}.

From (
4.23
), (
4.25
) and (
4.26
), we get that

\displaystyle\left|\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}(\partial_{x}u^{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}})\big|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{6}\langle\frac{1}{l}\rangle^{4}\omega^{\mathrm{L}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}+\langle t\rangle^{-1}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right).

(4.27)

Estimate of
\partial_{x}u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}
.

Similarly as (
4.23
), we infer that

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left|\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\left(\partial_{x}u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\mid\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right|

(4.28)

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{6}k\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}l\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)dkdld\xi d\eta\right|.

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\left|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}k\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}l\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{3}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)\right|dkdld\xi d\eta

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}\left|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}k\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\frac{\eta(k-l)-l(\xi-\eta)}{|l|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}}l\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\times\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)\right|dkdld\xi d\eta=:I_{k-l}^{\prime}+I_{l}^{\prime}.

For
I_{k-l}^{\prime}
, using (
4.21
) and (
4.10
),
we get that

\displaystyle I_{k-l}^{\prime}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}k\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{2}}\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{3}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{-2}\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}

(4.29)

\displaystyle\times\langle t\rangle\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\langle t\rangle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}.

For
I_{l}^{\prime}
, by (
4.21
) again, there holds that

\displaystyle I_{l}^{\prime}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle k,\xi+kt\rangle^{3}k\hat{\theta}_{k}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi)\|_{L_{k,\xi}^{2}}\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{2}\hat{\omega}_{l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\eta)\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}\|\langle l,\eta+lt\rangle^{-2}\|_{L_{l,\eta}^{2}}

(4.30)

\displaystyle\times\|\langle k-l,\xi-\eta+(k-l)t\rangle^{3}\left(|k-l|+|\xi-\eta|\right)\hat{\theta}_{k-l}^{\mathrm{NL}}(\xi-\eta)\|_{L_{k-l,\xi-\eta}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}.

Collecting (
4.28
), (
4.29
) and (
4.30
), we infer that

\displaystyle\left|\left(\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}(\partial_{x}u^{\mathrm{NL}}\cdot\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}})\big|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\partial_{x}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\omega^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\langle t\rangle\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\langle D_{x},D_{y}+tD_{x}\rangle^{3}\nabla\theta^{\mathrm{NL}}\|_{L^{2}}\right).

(4.31)
